# Renderer backend correspondence

The neutral operation IDs and families live only in
`src/video/renderer/gml_render_operations.def`. Private CPU and GPU ledgers are
`cpu/gml_render_cpu_operations.def` and `gpu/gml_render_gpu_operations.def`.
`tests/architecture/check_render_operations.py` checks one classification per ID,
real definitions for supported/fallback anchors, naming of the eight required
OpenGL counterparts, absence of fake definitions for planned entries, and the
live repair-level map's required fields, ledger status and source anchors.
`--require-delivered` rejects any planned entry; it is a final-delivery gate,
not the P1 CPU milestone. A planned entry cannot be selected. The historical
P1 CPU-only classification is retained below only as a dated architectural
snapshot; the live ledger and rule table here describe current support.

## Experimental scene fidelity contract

R03-SPR-INTEGER-FUNCTION extends ordinary packaged nearest sprites to both signs,
uniform tint, normal/disabled/inverse-source blending and integral physical scales across the
ordinary targets. Its paired matrix owns that extension. Older sprite
rows describe the limits of their cheaper retained recipes, not a global CPU
rejection of combinations separately qualified by this rule. Covered SCREEN or
application alpha needs at most one additional pass per compatible batch; it
never requires a predecessor snapshot or one draw per overlapping sprite.
The established modern numbered-target world nearest predecessor recipe remains
preferred after the cheaper existing integer crop refuses. This extension must
not shadow that separately guarded owner. Such unchanged predecessor controls
are counted explicitly in the paired generator and retain their documented
snapshot/batching debt; the zero-snapshot and eight-call grouping requirement
applies to the new route, not a qualification of that old recipe's replacement.
Magnified first-generation application sprites use this integer geometry owner
for fractional-origin partial alpha too: the retained float-phase preference
can lose trailing texel ties on larger cells. The paired late-text/large-sprite
control and complete first-generation sprite matrix guard that selection.
New fractional controls of that unchanged inherited recipe are explicit D8 debt,
not qualification of R03-SPR-INTEGER-FUNCTION. A D3D12 control with a negative
unit axis showed an EXACT-channel difference13; native verification remains
pending, so no driver-independent defect or tolerance waiver is inferred.
The generator's strict default rejects that difference. Its explicit debt mode
reports the old route's full-RGBA failures separately while retaining every
original D2 gate and strict D2 for all new/modified packets. Both the inherited
implementation and its numbered-world selection remain unchanged.
Raw-byte cutout discards alpha<=the reference before draw-alpha modulation;
all references0..255 are paired. Inverse RGB uses unattenuated integer-tinted
source colour, with zero/inverse-source-alpha blending on preserving targets.
Disabled inverse blending still replaces raw zero-alpha texels opaque unless
cutout discards them. Ordinary normal blending keeps its old zero-alpha discard.
Four disabled-blend controls use finite positive draw alpha1e-300, one per
normal/cutout/inverse/inverse-cutout state: exact source writes survive vertex
float underflow. Forty-eight exact ordered threshold/enable images qualify
observable batch splits; skipped-cutoff injection must fail. No ordinary reads,
destination snapshots or CPU float emulation are added by these states.
Inactive references are normalized only in executor snapshots, not readable
draw state. Ninety-six exact groups vary all256 inactive references in normal
and disabled inverse drawing: each stays in one driver draw without exhausting
the pending state's128-entry table, and the public reference remains255.

R03-TILE-CLASSIC-UNIT qualifies ordinary Classic positive-unit nearest tiles
on borrowed SCREEN and numbered targets. Integral contained packed source
selections and target coverage, opaque/binary alpha and white/full draw alpha
require EXACT RGBA0. The background-tile port keeps its shared source clipping;
single/room tile overruns remain unqualified. Empty selected/target coverage
preserves queued work. Owning on-demand authority, existing compatible nearest
batches and actual-pixel checkpoints add no shader, ordinary reader, destination
copy or CPU replay. Phase planes, filtering, tint, partial visible alpha, GUI/3D,
reflection and nonunit/fractional coverage retain debt. The classic-unit-tile
control pairs complete unchanged CPU/device RGBA and actual sealed no-current
bytes; whole-game fallback and speed qualification remain separate.

R03-TEXT-MAPPED-INTEGER extends the shared nearest crop only for opaque/binary
full-alpha black/white text at positive whole scales1..4. The text owner already resolved
world mapping and snapped glyph anchors. Every newly admitted span requires
on-demand actual-pixel checkpoint authority. Integral target clipping retains the
full UV phase; the variant adds
no ordinary observation or CPU replay. R03-SPR-INTEGER-ROUNDED-NOOP retains
positive whole nearest sprites whose half-up rounded physical crop is empty,
including subpixel slivers outside the raster. Integral cameras exclude the
modern half-camera tie. Both require EXACT RGBA0. The mapped-integer-text device
control pairs the unchanged CPU raster, queued prefixes and empty sprite calls;
partial-alpha text, reflections, filtering, rotation and special states receive
no new qualification. Whole-game speed remains a separate gate.

R03-TILE-INTEGER-CLIP extends the existing crop to positive whole nearest tile
scales1..8, including already-resolved world mapping and target-edge clipping.
Opaque/binary source alpha, white RGB and full drawing alpha require EXACT RGBA0.
The original full quad preserves UV phase while the viewport clips enlarged
texels. Clipped nonunit geometry requires owning on-demand observation and keeps
actual device pixels; it adds no CPU replay implementation or ordinary read.
Immutable nearest TILE checkpoints share the existing compatible atlas batch.
Integral anchors and projected axes are bounded to65536, atlas axes to16384;
trim, fractional source/geometry, reflection, filtering, rotation, partial alpha
and special states gain no new admission. The `integer-tile-clipping` control
requires9216 complete-RGBA CPU/device pairs, one compatible driver draw, stale
shadows, zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/copies/replay and exact explicitly
sealed actual-no-current bytes. One-alpha-level corruption must fail.
Existing576 unit-source and288 background comparisons remain unchanged.
This is not whole-game, frontend-state or speed qualification.

R03-TILE-UNIT-SOURCE-CLIP clips integral positive-unit nearest tile selections
against the untrimmed packed page using the existing CPU/shared part-view.
Right/bottom overrun and empty source/destination selections are EXACT RGBA0;
visible results reuse the ordinary nearest batch without a destination copy,
observer, shader change or CPU replay. Reflected/scaled, fractional-source,
trimmed, filtered, rotated and special-state extensions remain unqualified.
Exact nearest immutable TILE operations share the existing coalescible atlas
span with BACKGROUND operations; a family label alone no longer drains it.
Source/state/target/version and capacity guards remain unchanged.
The `unit-tile-source-clipping` paired control requires576 complete-RGBA cases,
one compatible prefix/subject/suffix driver draw, unchanged empty-subject target
authority/version, stale CPU shadows, zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/copies/replay
and exact explicitly sealed cached bytes after actual context detachment.
Whole-game fallback and speed qualification remain separate.

GPU scene binding at load, Reset and context recreation prepares the common scene,
automatic-composition and fixed font programs, geometry handles and destination-copy capacity at the current
engine extent through `gml_gpu_prepare_scene`. CPU scene selection does not call it.
Preparation requires accessible owning-context dispatch. Once per context, it executes the existing
terminal and automatic-composition pipelines into a private 1x1 target and waits during preparation, not gameplay: linking alone
need not finish driver compilation. The real frontend target is restored, never written or read;
the tiny upload and both draws are counted, without scene-execution credit or published image authority.
The composition writer reuses its ordered device-local predecessor copy, never a CPU observation.
Repeated bindings reuse capacity; context loss forgets it. Content programs and larger future extents
retain their existing demand-driven paths. Preparation failure is nonfatal and retains ordinary
lazy retry. Raster rules, EXACT/TOLERANCE classifications and state bytes are unchanged.

Room warming at initial start, Reset and room entry additionally prepares GPU copies of its
already decoded sprite/background pages. Packaged real/sprite-font pages are decoded/uploaded once
per distinct page at each boundary, within a 64 MiB aggregate speculative budget; the device
reuses existing immutable identity/version uploads. Fonts have priority; remaining budget also prepares
immutable pages whose first users are not yet live room instances. Mutable/runtime-rasterized pages, unsupported extents,
missing current-context proof and failures keep ordinary lazy paths. CPU warming is unchanged;
the sprite/background helpers do not upload mask-only pages solely for collision use. This is resource preparation, not
new draw admission, fallback exemption or zero first-use cost for every future content resource.

R03-P9-AUTOMATIC-UNDERLAY also prepares an already queued underlay before an
otherwise qualified public stretched application-surface GUI draw with zero camera offset.
Changing automatic-draw enablement does not cancel that queued intent. The
existing compositor and its one device-local destination snapshot are unchanged;
the following surface uses its existing hardware sampling and fixed blend path.
No CPU leaf, shader arithmetic, observer or replay policy is added. Non-GUI,
nonzero-camera, numbered-source and the compositor's existing source/state exclusions retain CPU.
The paired `surface-underlay-order` control exercises both Studio policies,
CPU/device-current sources, GUI mapping, clipping and opaque/partial suffixes:
16 EXACT RGBA0 and16 TOLERANCE±2 controls,32 reversed-order negative controls,
zero ordinary reads/masks/queries/replay and exact sealed no-current pixels.

R03-P5-GUI-FULLWIDTH extends first-generation GUI point presentation and corrected
1:1 partial-alpha composition with interpolation off/on.
It is EXACT for qualified integer opaque point geometry and TOLERANCE±2 for
individual noninteger geometry or partial alpha, with exact coverage in both categories.

R03-P5-GUI-OPAQUE-SUFFIX preserves SCREEN's known-opaque metadata after a
qualified opaque-source GUI point presentation or partial-alpha unit composition
whose rounded/clipped rectangle covers the whole destination. These existing
leaves write opaque alpha at every covered pixel. No pixels are inspected and
no sampling, blending or CPU raster implementation changes. Partial coverage,
nonunit partial-alpha and other states gain no new proof. This enables an
already-qualified partial-SCREEN box-filtered sprite suffix without a CPU barrier.
Its additional 240 authored controls require EXACT0 for opaque point prefixes
and outside-coverage bytes, TOLERANCE±2 for partial prefixes and identical-input
single suffix draws, and the independent composed±4 bound. Explicit test-only
prefix observation is excluded from the separately checked zero ordinary reads;
actual device bytes are sealed before exact no-current recovery.

Resident begin/loss causes are recorded only under the existing opt-in dispatch
diagnostics; see [resolved draw dispatch accounting](DIAGNOSTICS.md#resolved-draw-dispatch-accounting).
The per-public-frame census separates admission skips, begin predicates and the
first CPU execution that loses residency from draw volume. This metadata changes
no raster rule, EXACT/TOLERANCE classification or CPU reference behavior; a cause
count alone is not GPU execution or performance qualification.

The R03-P5-SURFACE-STRETCHED application-to-SCREEN candidate admits the
content-owned first-generation opaque GUI point-presentation leaf and ordinary
filtered magnified alpha/tint draws. Point coverage uses destination pixel
centres; hardware nearest copies RGB and writes opaque alpha. Qualified 1:1 or
integer point geometry remains EXACT; fractional point and hardware-bilinear
single draws require TOLERANCE±2. Both use fixed-function source-over and
versioned application sources, without predecessor copies or CPU replay.
Resource/backend call controls cover admission, clipping, refusal and capacity
batching. The first-generation GUI full-width extension below retains the CPU
source-aspect correction before point or partial-alpha unit preparation,
including centred width and width-capped height. Its paired complete-RGBA controls cover CPU-current and
device-rendered sources, GUI mapping, clipping, exact outside coverage and sealed
actual-no-current recovery. Other full-width states retain ordered CPU fallback;
whole-workload and speed qualification remain separate.
An admitted complete nearest replacement from a proven opaque atlas page at full
draw alpha restores the application-opacity metadata discarded by generic enqueue.
Partial coverage, binary/partial source alpha and partial draw alpha do not gain
that proof. This enables device-produced opaque application point presentation
without a source readback; CPU drawing bodies and expectations remain unchanged.

The identity SCREEN GUI extension of R03-SPR-COARSE-MIP, with interpolation
enabled or disabled as in the unchanged full-target CPU leaf, uses fixed-function
source-over, with EXACT zero/full-alpha endpoints and TOLERANCE±2 partial-alpha
single draws. Admission requires default fragment state,
full minified coverage and a positive owning-context on-demand witness. The
existing four-byte coarse-texel preparation/upload remains; the new GUI branch
needs no predecessor snapshot or CPU replay retention and preserves actual device
bytes only on observation. Unknown or nonopaque SCREEN destinations reuse the
hardware-sampled, batched alpha-only coverage pass: a positive full-target veil
writes opaque alpha as the unchanged CPU leaf does; a zero-alpha veil preserves
the destination. Known-opaque destinations keep their single-pass path.
Nonidentity GUI and unavailable witnesses retain the
ordered CPU path. `test_render_resources::resident_gui_coarse_veil_on_demand`
checks authority, witness refusal and explicit observation; the real-device
R03-SPR-COARSE-MIP GUI comparator checks RGBA, no ordinary reads/copies and exact
sealed recovery. The world branch and its exact comparison assertions remain
unchanged; no whole-game, all-GUI or speed qualification follows from this slice.

R03-SPR-GUI-MAPPED-STRETCH admits previously excluded centred/mapped SCREEN GUI
magnification of an untrimmed immutable atlas frame. Shared GUI mapping, camera
subtraction, floored origin and rounded extent match the unchanged CPU entry.
White/full drawing alpha, ordinary fragment state, positive enlargement on both
axes, a witnessed owning context and bounded finite geometry are required.
The existing hardware bilinear surface sampler clamps physical crop edges and
uses fixed source-over plus batched opaque-written-alpha coverage. Individual
draws are TOLERANCE±2; zero-alpha sources and outside coverage remain EXACT0.
There are no destination snapshots, CPU float-emulation shaders, ordinary reads
or retained replay recipes. Explicit observation seals actual GPU bytes before
no-current-context recovery. Existing identity/mapped-zero-camera exact paths,
trim/minification, tint, partial drawing alpha and special-state exclusions are
unchanged. Synthetic overlap controls prove batching, not accumulated fidelity
or whole-game speed.

CPU remains the default, exact reference. The optional GPU scene backend uses
EXACT for the qualified cheap exact rules and TOLERANCE±2 for an individual
filtered or partially blended draw with identical inputs. A composed real-workload
image permits TOLERANCE±4 per eight-bit RGBA channel at every pixel. Geometry,
coverage, draw order, runtime state and restoration of actual saved GPU pixels
are not relaxed. TOLERANCE±2 labels below describe individual draws; older
composed±2 qualification notes are historical evidence, not the current gate.
Synthetic repeated-surface accumulation is informative unless drift greater than
sixteen levels is demonstrated in a pattern used by an actual workload.

The same 864 physical controls on native NVIDIA Core OpenGL and llvmpipe
GLES/Core OpenGL preserve the exact clear/copy control and keep all 288 single
draws within one level in RGBA. Known synthetic accumulation maxima are
RGBA17/13/28/0 on native NVIDIA and RGBA32/21/32/0 on llvmpipe; the historical
composed±2 assertion fails 228 and 284 controls respectively. This is not solely
a D3D12 translation-layer effect. Four full-raster real-workload checkpoints on
each driver family remain within three RGB levels, meeting the composed±4 bound.
CPU frontend XRGB does not define alpha, so those checkpoints do not qualify
persistent-image alpha or every scene variant. Synthetic RGBA proof and exact
state restoration controls remain distinct from frontend-visible RGB evidence.

An additional full-output CRT workload comparison at 3440-by-1600 has one red
pixel five levels from CPU at its transition checkpoint on both native NVIDIA
and D3D12. Its other two checkpoints meet the four-level composed bound. This
known experimental deviation is not specific to the translation layer, does
not qualify the complete route, and does not change the per-pixel contract.

The empty-screen recovery operation reduces exact RGB-zero predicates to two
RGBA8 device flags, then conditionally draws the application image with the
existing leading-edge point geometry and fixed RGB blending. It neither assumes
a content shader drew nonblack pixels nor removes recovery when it did not.
Only plain Studio magnification on a witnessed resident SCREEN is admitted,
with either texture-interpolation setting: both CPU branches use the same
leading-edge point indices for magnification. Reductions do not share that rule;
classic, active-program, full-width, reduction and other state variants retain
the old query/recovery path. The device-only conditional image is observed for
state or controlled destruction, not retained as a CPU replay recipe.

Qualified magnified numbered-source composition is still experimental, not a
general claim of zero-transfer rendering. Unqualified geometry, source/state
variants, CPU content consumers and absent context-ownership proof retain their
established fallback. Boot-route read/mask/transport counters and single-pair
timings do not establish an ordinary-gameplay per-frame transfer budget. The
hardware additive descriptor remains backend-only; this contract does not itself
admit public additive sprite variants without workload qualification.

For checkpoint timing, `src/core/engine.c::anygm_run_frame` measures the final
`gml_render_scene_flush` as the existing profile's `seal` phase. The optional
adapter trace in `src/adapters/libretro/libretro_entry.c::retro_run` measures
the entire loaded frontend tick and callback envelopes, including that seal.
Its `retro_serialize_size`, `retro_serialize` and `retro_unserialize` boundaries
also report actual state-call results and CPU-visible costs through
`state_profile_begin` / `state_profile_report`, without changing state transport
or claiming frontend ring acceptance.
See [call-boundary profiling](LIBRETRO.md#optional-call-boundary-profiling) for
the exact clock, cadence and GPU-completion exclusions and neutral controls.
This instrumentation does not change any rule, shader or authority boundary
mapped below; checkpoint bytes remain product cost, not test overhead.

Retained SCREEN/application canonical checkpoints may use explicit-baseline
incremental reads (R03-P9-INCREMENTAL-CHECKPOINT). The backend compares current
RGBA8 pixels with the previous successful GPU checkpoint using exact texel
fetches, downloads a small 32-by-32 tile change mask and then one union rectangle
of changed pixels. The existing host row/channel converter packs that region;
the resource publishes only that rectangle after complete success. An unchanged
image publishes nothing. This preserves a complete current CPU-canonical frame
before frontend return; it does not eliminate all CPU data or observer cost.
Ordinary observers, cold/stale baselines, numbered targets, changed extents or
formats, unsupported detection, dense changes and targets above the independent
32-MiB GPU baseline limit retain full readback. Two baseline textures and one
small shared mask are context-local, bounded, disposable and never state bytes.
`readbacks`/`readback_bytes` count actual attempted pixel reads, including failed
ones; `checkpoint_masks`/`checkpoint_mask_bytes` separately count actual mask
reads. Logical checkpoint success is still resource-owned. Correctness controls
do not establish a whole-route transfer reduction or performance gain.

Completed-picture save preparation is owned by
`src/core/engine_state.c::state_write_completed_frame` and `::state_write`,
not a shader or raster executor. `::engine_state_save` permits an early stop
only after both existing encoding lower bounds exceed the supplied capacity;
the established frame-free writer then supplies the same bytes. A failed
compact attempt restores the original complete-plus-compact failure path.
CPU `::engine_state_size` and all fitting complete writes retain the full codec.
The comparative owner is
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::completed_frame_capacity_case`;
fixed/variable transport and non-current-context rewind are additionally
covered by the existing libretro state contracts. This CPU codec optimization
does not remove GPU checkpoints or change their authority/residency rule.

Active scene GPU size queries use `src/core/engine_state.c::state_size_is_metadata_only` and
`common/gml_render_state.c::gml_render_state_capacity`: metadata-only raw completed-frame and
inline-sprite ceilings, plus the existing surface RLE worst case. They neither observe device
pixels nor replay a frame or prepare pixel-compression caches. The actual save codec and
transactional rollback still consume exact canonical bytes, using the writer's payload length
rather than the allocation bound. CPU sizing remains exact. The controls are
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_size_query_is_metadata_only_case`,
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::metadata_state_capacity_preserves_pending_pixels`
and `::metadata_state_capacity_skips_pixel_compression`; the frontend observation-cost control
retains exact saved-byte, image/audio, fixed/variable ring and no-current-context checks.
This is a sizing prerequisite, not on-demand GPU preservation, volatile loss or removal of ordinary
replay/checkpoints. No raster rule or its EXACT/TOLERANCE classification changes.

The explicit state observer is `common/gml_render_resources.c::gml_render_resource_observe_device`,
using the existing staged checkpoint body without substituting a replay recipe.
`common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_observe_device_resources` drains pending work and
observes dirty numbered/application/owned SCREEN resources. The root writer calls it only after
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_is_current` receives the host's positive owning-thread
proof. An absent witness retains the existing resource writer. Negative installed proof rejects
unobserved images before CPU replay, using the metadata-only
`common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_device_observation_pending`; already observed CPU
images remain usable. Size queries never ask for that proof.
The adapter may resolve a thread-local platform getter through the frontend and compare the exact
adopted context identity at each observation; resolving GL entry points alone grants no access.
The controls are `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_state_observes_lent_device_case`,
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::device_observation_preserves_actual_replayable_pixels`
and `tests/contract/libretro_hardware_render.c::context_access_proof_tracks_exact_owner`.
They cover actual RGBA instead of a different synthetic replay response, failed-read publication,
unbound/foreign/replaced contexts and unchanged context-free state bytes. This does not certify
every frontend configuration.
Witnessed GPU scene services opt into `gml_gpu.c::scene_observe_on_demand` through
`gml_render_device.h::GmlRenderDeviceServices`. Successful resource submissions then publish
`GML_RENDER_RESOURCE_GPU_RESIDENT` without retaining operation journals, mutable source copies
or replay recipes, even for an EXACT operation. Consecutive draws and byte-free resident sources
keep actual device authority until a CPU/state observer; temporary observation failure preserves
that version for retry. Proven pre-write refusal observes an earlier resident prefix before
ordered CPU fallback; uncertain writes invalidate it instead of replaying stale CPU pixels.
Actual context loss invalidates unobserved resident resources without calling the dead device.
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_bind_renderer` enables this policy only for GPU scenes;
CPU scenes, absent witnesses and negative initial proofs retain their established journal policy.
The controls are `tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_authority_avoids_replay_retention`,
`::resident_observation_retry_and_fallback`, `::resident_source_copy_and_loss` and the existing
engine state/loss controls below. EXACT/TOLERANCE raster classification and CPU kernels are unchanged.
Content CPU fallbacks still require observation; this is not a zero-read or performance claim.
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_finish_scene` uses
`gml_render.c::gml_render_scene_submit_pending` for witnessed current scene passes instead of
the all-resource checkpoint seal, including world, GUI, multiview and final frame boundaries.
Existing CPU-reader barriers remain; CPU scenes and absent-witness hosts keep the established
seal. `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_frame_end_defers_observation_case` keeps a
pending persistent image across three frames without a checkpoint/replay, then saves/restores
the actual GPU RGBA; its absent-witness control still seals each frame.
`tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::unbound_context_access_case` checks desktop/embedded refusal
before upload, readback, submit, flush, wait, synchronous query and terminal-plan graphics calls,
with rebind/retry and initial-adoption refusal. Cached capabilities, program failures and logical
retirement remain metadata-only; ordinary GL work requires positive installed proof when present.
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_state_unbound_observation_is_retryable_case`
also preserves resource/frame authority and caller bytes across unavailable complete, frame-free
and rollback observations, then rebinds and restores actual RGBA.
`::scene_destroy_observation_fault_case` covers negative proof, one bounded transient retry and
persistent read failure. The latter reports loss and invalidates still-unobserved volatile pixels
rather than fabricating GPU bytes. Healthy controlled destruction preserves the actual pixels.

Completed-frame transport is EXACT for the actual GPU input RGBA, not a claim that a mixed scene
matches the CPU raster byte for byte. `src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_present_hardware_screen`
captures eligible scene output with positive context proof through
`gpu/gml_gpu.c::gml_gpu_capture_completed_frame`; `gpu/opengl/gml_render_gpu_opengl_presentation.c`
owns a separate snapshot texture/FBO, extent and generation, never the ordinary shader readback target.
Capture performs GPU-to-GPU copy only. `src/core/engine_presentation.c::engine_materialize_completed_frame`
reads this snapshot on observation and `gml_render.c::gml_render_completed_screen_publish` retires the
earlier SCREEN recipe after success. The existing row/channel conversion is reused; failed reads
publish nothing and stale/unbound/lost contexts are refused. Controlled current-context destruction
observes resources and the completed image before deletion. `GmlGpuCounters.completed_frame_*`
separates this traffic from scene checkpoints and content reads. The controls are
`tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::completed_frame_snapshot_case` and
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_state_preserves_completed_device_case`.
Missing proof, excluded presentation variants and ordinary replay/checkpoint policy retain their
existing paths. Context-access transitions, full frontend qualification and removal of ordinary
maintenance remain open; this transport adds no raster family.

Scene-mode genuine loss is owned by `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_lose_volatile_device`
and `src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_graphics_lose_scene_context`. Device-authoritative numbered
surfaces and targets of discarded queued work become nonexistent; current CPU/state images
survive. Existing surface-free target unwinding and incarnation allocation are reused. Managed
application storage is reinitialised only when unobserved; lost completed device images are
withdrawn rather than replayed. No dead-context callback, checkpoint or deletion is permitted.
The controls are `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::scene_volatile_context_loss_case`,
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::volatile_loss_discards_queued_nested_target`
and `::volatile_loss_recreates_only_unobserved_application`. These are lifecycle controls, not
new raster coverage, ordinary-read removal or whole-frontend qualification. CPU-mode context
recovery, initial adoption, Reset and valid saved-image restoration keep their existing paths.

The later resident prototype supports clear, qualified whole-image quads, and nearest-filtered
interior atlas subrectangles with integer-sized axis-aligned destinations and optional X/Y mirrors.
A narrow ordinary sprite path accepts unit-scale, integer-aligned authored trim when its atlas crop
has only alpha 0 or 255; other texture-stage filters are irrelevant to this unprogrammed base-image
draw, and a binary-alpha crop uses endpoint source-over. Its CPU journal replay rejects any
intermediate-alpha source. These variants are replayable from retained source pixels after context
loss. A separate first-generation filtered path accepts qualified ordinary sprites with
partial alpha, tint, per-draw alpha, authored trim, partial source crops and either scale sign. It preserves snapped
destination coverage and fractional texture phase. For logical padding around a packed atlas
cell, taps crossing a padded edge use physical atlas coordinates on both axes; other taps clamp to
the selected source rectangle. The path requires physical margin pixels where needed, uses
source-over RGB with the application's source-alpha-squared coverage rule, and checkpoints the
device result rather than claiming exact CPU replay. Qualified cardinal and arbitrary rotations
use the same source-over checkpoint with the first-generation CPU's nearest-texel inverse map;
near-zero angles classified as axis draws, special sprite states and unqualified crop geometry
retain the ordered CPU fallback. A separate modern-layer filtered rotation branch uses the CPU's
four-tap inverse map, nonzero-alpha crop bounds and conditional per-row quadrilateral span. It
requires a proven opaque application target and uses the modern screen target's opaque-alpha
blend policy; transparent targets still cross the ordered CPU barrier. Both branches retain
the CPU checkpoint for lost-device reconstruction.
The public `draw_sprite_general` entry selects its source part independently of the authored
sprite origin. Uniform-corner rotation uses the existing rotated CPU blitter or the qualified
resident inverse-map quad. Distinct corner colours use a CPU two-triangle colour interpolation
path; an ordinary atlas selection, including one crossing logical trim, can instead submit
the same two transformed, vertex-coloured triangles on GPU. The authored rectangle defines
geometry and colour interpolation while the whole packed page defines filtered sampling.
The selected-source and colour variants preserve ordered CPU fallbacks for runtime sprites,
nondefault blend and other unqualified states. A first-generation cardinal source edge exactly on a pixel
centre also falls back: its incremental fixed-point CPU row walk can choose a different texel
from an independently sampled GPU fragment. These are bounded ordinary-scene qualifications,
not a claim that arbitrary surface, shader, GUI or skeletal draws are resident.
Ordinary magnified `draw_sprite_stretched` atlas draws in first-generation and modern-layer
policies have a separate bounded filtered path:
it uses the CPU leaf's floored destination origin and rounded extent, preserves the full logical
cell around authored trim, and returns transparent samples only on authored margin sides.
It requires a proven opaque application target, bilinear sampling, positive magnification on
both axes and physical atlas space for the full logical quad. Transparent targets, special
blend state, out-of-atlas logical cells and deep minification retain ordered CPU execution.
The full-target coarse-mip effect has its own qualified rule below; it is not an ordinary
bilinear magnification. This qualification does not cover nine-slice or surfaces.
Ordinary non-full-target minification and mixed-axis stretches use a separate bounded box-filter
quad: each destination pixel averages the same integer source box as the CPU, excluding authored
trim texels from both the sum and divisor. The device checkpoint preserves the exact result
without a context. The first-generation and modern-layer paths accept up to eight covered
source texels per axis (64 taps), a proven opaque destination and ordinary blend state, with
or without global interpolation. Deeper boxes remain ordered CPU fallbacks. The full-target
coarse-mip veil is prepared and composed by a separate effect rule, not this per-pixel box sample.
The public `draw_sprite_pos` entry can keep an ordinary axis-aligned atlas rectangle resident
through the existing nearest or bilinear cell preparation. Its complete, cropped, trimmed,
binary-alpha and filtered/intermediate-alpha forms preserve the mapped corner geometry; the
filtered first-generation and modern-layer paths retain their distinct target-alpha and
fractional-edge rules, including the modern half-camera tie. A separate checkpointed quad
keeps convex non-axis atlas pages resident for ordinary source-over: affine parallelograms use
the CPU triangles' common inverse map, while genuinely warped forms interpolate local page
coordinates across the same two triangles. Nearest and bilinear sampling, clipped edges,
authored crops, tint-free draw alpha and intermediate source alpha retain their characterized
CPU pixels. Non-convex, self-intersecting or special-blend forms remain on the ordered CPU
path. Fractional non-affine coordinates, freely rotated nearest affine edges and nearest
warps without horizontal/vertical leading edges are conservatively excluded pending exact
pixel-centre tie rules; the corresponding filtered forms are qualified. Modern-layer
intermediate-alpha draws require a proven opaque target; first-generation source-over can
also preserve a translucent target. This does not make surface, shader, GUI or skeletal
positioned draws resident.
At the P3 boundary, CPU text resolved a preceding resident sprite before writing glyph pixels.
The live P6 rules below now admit qualified packaged and versioned runtime glyphs to GPU; a
bounded P9 rule also admits filtered, reduced immutable real-font glyphs. Both preserve the
ordered CPU barrier for excluded variants. A similarly bounded first-generation
nearest background path accepts complete untrimmed pages at unit positive scale with intermediate
alpha. It checkpoints the device's source-over
result, treats only the low RGB 24 bits of a GML white tint as colour, and retains the existing
binary-alpha replay path unchanged. A resident-only background draw whose full untrimmed source
lies conservatively outside either target axis writes no pixels and does not force a CPU barrier;
this does not qualify visible mirrors or trimmed backgrounds. A terminal automatic
application-surface underlay may also use the exact current resident
image: the existing plan's integer nearest axes select the pixels, while the GL executor reads the
scene texture directly without a CPU upload. This requires an opaque, owned application surface,
no intervening GUI draw, and the guarded prototype setting. A failed or lost device reconstructs
the source from the resource journal before the existing CPU presentation writes it. Arbitrary
rotation and GUI draws over the underlay remain unqualified and use the CPU fallback. This narrow terminal path
does not establish a no-full-frame-transfer route for general gameplay.

The narrow ordinary-sprite route now copies consecutive compatible textured-quad
descriptors into one renderer-owned, bounded (2,048-operation) pending batch. It
keeps immutable atlas sources borrowed, projects each target version in draw
order, and submits at a full queue, CPU pixel borrow, target/lifecycle boundary,
or the end of the world pass. Qualified immutable sprite/text checkpoint quads may
span up to 128 distinct draw states and 64 atlas sources in one submission; other
source/state changes still close the batch. The existing
resource authority and CPU replay path still own partial device failure and
context-loss recovery. A successful full-target clear supersedes the older
replay journal only after the device accepts that clear; an unsuccessful clear
retains the older journal for fallback. A deferred resident clear projects its
next target version through the queued prefix before submission, preserving
EXACT order without a CPU pixel borrow. The neutral resource control
`resident_pending_clear_after_queued_draw` covers application, SCREEN and numbered
targets with exact replay and on-demand authority. These optimizations do not broaden the
set of qualified scene operations or change CPU raster bodies.

## Current operation support and repair-level rules

Forced-aspect owned admission preserves EXACT target placement and coverage;
each draw retains its existing EXACT or TOLERANCE±2 sampling/blending rule.
With OpenGL selected, one nonclassic, noncomposed view may use the resident
owned application when an existing declared surface canvas already selects it,
or when its full-origin view fills a same-sized forced application raster.
This reuses CPU's existing full-raster forced-view composition, not a new fit.
The resident SCREEN pass follows only a successful resident world begin and
retains the centered-GUI exclusion. Narrow-room masks, translated ports, live
assigned view surfaces, multiple views and ineligible contexts keep their
ordered CPU paths; those ordinary fallbacks remain unfinished GPU coverage,
not newly documented rare exceptions. No raster kernel or state schema changes.
`tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::forced_owned_scene_case` compares
complete CPU/selected images and positively checks scene admission plus the
narrow-room exclusion. The same neutral fixture is exported by
`test_gpu_scene_route --keep-forced-owned` and `--keep-forced-narrow` for real
device comparison. Recording-driver success alone is not device pixel,
ordinary-transfer or whole-route performance qualification.

The single-view coordinator also selects the existing resident base preparation
before entering a native-sized numbered `view_surface_id` target. This applies
only to non-classic, non-composed, non-forced-aspect frames with one visible view,
matching world and surface dimensions, an active device and selected OpenGL scene
rendering. The view retains its resolved room camera and the ordinary numbered
target facade; unsupported draws still take their existing ordered CPU barrier.
After sealing, only a drawn base is immediately observed for CPU composition;
an assigned destination remains resource-owned until an actual consumer reads it.
Resized assignments and absent or ineligible contexts retain the established CPU
path. No CPU raster, shader, state schema, multiview compositor or sampling rule
changes. `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::resident_native_assigned_view_case`
checks admission with application drawing enabled and disabled, a displaced camera,
resized-target exclusion, exact canonical state crossing, completed-frame restoration
and context loss/recreation. Its fake driver is not real-device pixel or speed proof.

The experimental magnified full-source numbered-surface rule is TOLERANCE±2 per draw,
with the composed-image contract and remaining RGBA limitations above.
`common/gml_render_surfaces.c::resident_surface_filtered` uses the unchanged
CPU stretched rectangle's floor/round geometry, metadata-only current source views, and the
existing versioned target queue. CPU source writes, target switches, resize, free, and renderer
teardown flush before borrowed source storage can change. `gpu/opengl/gml_gpu_opengl_batch.c::hardware_surface_quad_valid`
requires ordinary source-over, full-source hardware bilinear magnification and matching opaque
written-alpha coverage; `::flush_quads` uses one RGB draw plus one alpha-only draw per compatible
batch, without destination snapshots. `gpu/opengl/gml_gpu_opengl_shaders.h::R03_SHADER_HARDWARE_FILTERED_SURFACE`
uses hardware filtering and no CPU-float emulation. The unchanged CPU comparison owner is
`cpu/gml_render_cpu_blit_internal.h::surf_bi_band`. `tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::scene_hardware_surface_batch_case`
checks capacity, separate functions and pre-write exclusion; `tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::filtered_surface_pending_source_lifetime`
checks write/resize/free/target/teardown boundaries and retained retry after a pre-write refusal.
These call/lifetime checks do not themselves establish composed pixel, whole-route or performance coverage.
Application sources, reduction, mirrors, active shaders and special blend state keep their existing paths.

`common/gml_render_sprites.c::resident_filtered_atlas_page` checks finite normalised
atlas UV coordinates within [0,1] before enqueuing an axis-aligned candidate. A valid physical
source rectangle does not guarantee this after fractional coverage rounding. Unqualified
coordinates retain the ordered CPU path; they are not clamped or admitted into an immutable
batch which the device would refuse. `tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_atlas_uv_refusal_preserves_queued_prefix`
checks all four atlas boundaries and successful completion of the preceding valid queued draw.

This is the live operation-ledger view, distinct from the P1 snapshot below.
`TEXTURED_QUAD` also carries the already-qualified two-triangle positioned and
corner-colour geometry; the separate `TEXTURED_TRIANGLES` opcode is not selected.
The terminal resident-source optimization belongs to the existing render plan,
not to a selected neutral `PRESENT_IMAGE` scene opcode. The four unselected
scene entries name their concrete CPU fallback rather than promising separate
future executors. Their qualified quad/plan alternatives remain mapped below;
classifying the fallback does not establish ordinary-route GPU residency.

| Neutral ID | Current GPU ledger | Current interpretation |
|---|---|---|
| `TEXTURED_QUAD` | `supported` | Only the bounded sprite/background/tile/glyph and opaque or binary-alpha integer surface-part/stretched rule groups below; an unqualified public draw uses an ordered CPU barrier. |
| `TEXTURED_TRIANGLES` | `fallback` | No separate selected executor. Qualified positioned/corner-colour geometry is a textured quad; unqualified positioned geometry uses the preserved CPU triangle raster. |
| `COLOURED_PRIMITIVE` | `supported` | P6 opaque flat, circle/triangle span, line, outline and particle-square pixel regions; unqualified geometry retains the ordered CPU bridge. |
| `CLEAR` | `supported` | Qualified resident target clear. |
| `COPY_IMAGE` | `fallback` | Public full/partial raw surface copies use clipped alias-safe CPU kernels and coherent versioned borrows. The resident prototype copy is a distinct qualified textured quad, not an accelerated public copy API. |
| `COMPOSE_SURFACE` | `fallback` | Unqualified surface composition uses ordered CPU kernels. Qualified opaque/binary integer public part/stretched rules use `TEXTURED_QUAD`; qualified full-target program draws use `CONTENT_SHADER_DRAW`. |
| `LAYER_EFFECT` | `supported` | P7 attached room-layer tint, colourise and zoom blur plus qualified inline RGB noise use GPU arithmetic after a CPU-current capture and device checkpoint. Other filter kinds and standalone tint retain CPU fallback. This does not claim zero full-target transfer or whole-game speed. |
| `PRESENT_IMAGE` | `fallback` | The terminal resident-source optimization belongs to the existing plan; other presentation uses coherent CPU materialization. No selected neutral scene-op or zero-transfer gameplay claim. |
| `CONTENT_SHADER_DRAW` | `supported` | Qualified full-target programs preserve per-draw uniforms/samplers and distinct-target multipass without an intermediate checkpoint. Excluded target/program variants retain their mapped ordered fallback; there is no exact software shader interpreter. |
| `SOFTWARE3D_PASS` | `fallback` | Ordered CPU bridge, not accelerated 3D. |
| `RECOVER_EMPTY_SCREEN` | `supported` | Plain Studio magnified application recovery, including identity full-width recovery with zero camera, no world transform and identical application/aspect/SCREEN extents: EXACT global RGB-zero predicate and leading-edge point placement; opaque/binary draws EXACT, partial RGB blending TOLERANCE±2. GPU reduction flags gate the blit without a result query or pixel read. Other variants retain the existing query/recovery owner. `conditional_screen_recovery_fullwidth_authority` checks admission and pre-write refusals separately from real-device pixel qualification. |

`gpu/opengl/gml_gpu_opengl_resources.c::gml_gpu_opengl_scene_capabilities`
advertises the six selected opcode bits and their neutral TEXTURE, PRIMITIVE,
SURFACE, EFFECT and SHADER families. Family bits describe the catalogue's opcodes, not
public-draw counter attribution: a SURFACE-attributed textured quad does not advertise
`COPY_IMAGE` or `COMPOSE_SURFACE`. The exact masks and transactional refusal of
all four unselected opcodes are checked by
`tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::scene_service_contract_case`, including a valid
clear prefix which must not execute before an unsupported suffix is rejected.

The anchors below use `path::symbol` relative to `src/video/renderer/`, except
`tests/` paths, which are relative to the repository root. They name actual
functions or stable shader-branch labels in source. A shader label identifies
the fragment or vertex arithmetic, not an invented executor. Rules may share
one hot quad kernel. The comparison column names a core-owned discriminator
where one exists; the same stable rule ID identifies additional device/CPU
comparisons in the external test owner's record. `pending` means the named
variant still lacks complete comparison evidence, not that registry coverage
proves pixel equivalence. The shared column owns source, geometry, eligibility
and draw-state decisions; the CPU and GPU columns own their respective pixel
rounding/sampling. Special shaders, surfaces, GUI, software3d and unqualified
blend/coverage are ordered CPU fallbacks unless a row expressly qualifies them.

The layer capture still crosses the CPU-current boundary through
`common/gml_render_effects.c::gml_render_layer_filter_begin`,
`gml_render.c::gml_render_resident_application_cpu_barrier` and, for numbered
targets, `cpu/gml_render_cpu_surfaces.c::surface_pixels`. The recognized
tint/colourise path below uploads this unfiltered capture, applies a real device
pixel rule and checkpoints the result. Unrecognized filter families continue
through `cpu/gml_render_cpu_effects.c::gml_render_cpu_layer_filter_end`; the
capture barrier comparison is
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_layer_filter_capture_barrier`.
Neither path establishes zero-transfer effect composition.

`R03-PRIM-CLASSIC-ALPHA-RECT` is `TOLERANCE±2`: filled, uniform-colour rectangles
with finite partial alpha on the known-opaque borrowed early-classic SCREEN retain
their inclusive CPU raster bounds and use batched, source-free fixed source-over.
The owning-context observation witness is required. Numbered targets, unknown or
nonopaque destination alpha, outlines, gradients and other blend states retain CPU
fallback. No destination predecessor, pixel read or float-arithmetic emulation is
required during drawing; observations preserve the actual produced image. Neutral
controls are `resident_classic_alpha_rectangle_scope` and
`scene_classic_alpha_rect_batch`; the latter checks the quad-capacity boundary and
transactional rejection. CPU fallback reuses the existing rectangle raster body.

`R03-PRIM-CLASSIC-INVERSE-RECT` is `TOLERANCE±2` for RGB, with exact coverage
and opaque alpha: uniform filled rectangles using the inverse-source-colour preset
at drawing alpha 1 on the same known-opaque borrowed early-classic SCREEN use
`GL_ZERO, GL_ONE_MINUS_SRC_COLOR` for RGB and `GL_ONE, GL_ZERO` for alpha.
The existing source-free rectangle shader and inclusive CPU bounds are reused.
Compatible rectangles batch to quad capacity; changing to source-over splits the
batch. No destination snapshot, ordinary read or CPU float emulation is needed.
Numbered targets, partial drawing alpha, outlines, gradients, unknown/nonopaque
target alpha and other special states remain CPU debt, not exceptions.
`common/gml_render_primitives.c::resident_classic_alpha_rect` prepares the packet;
`common/gml_render_draw.c::gml_render_classic_inverse_rect_valid` checks its envelope;
`gpu/opengl/gml_gpu_opengl_batch.c::gml_gpu_opengl_scene_submit` executes it.
Fallback still uses `cpu/gml_render_cpu_primitives.c::gml_render_cpu_rectangle`.
Core comparisons are `resident_classic_inverse_rectangle_scope` and
`scene_classic_inverse_rect_batch`; the real-device comparison uses public
preparation, single draws, eight overlapping draws and explicitly sealed actual
pixels. Synthetic accumulation is diagnostic under the owner contract; it does
not qualify a real composed image or a whole game.

`R03-PRIM-STUDIO-ALPHA-RECT` is `TOLERANCE±2` for individual RGB, with
`EXACT` covered alpha and outside pixels. First-generation Studio filled uniform
rectangles on an ordinary SCREEN or owned application use fixed source-over RGB
and one coverage-only opaque-alpha pass per compatible quad group. The unchanged
CPU raster quantizes partial alpha; the GPU does not emulate that arithmetic.
Resolved inclusive raster bounds are clipped once, without applying world scale
again. Drawing alpha must be at least 1/256 and below one. Pending underlay
composition, modern ROOM layers, nonidentity GUI, numbered/stacked targets, gradients, outlines, custom state and shaders
remain ordinary CPU debt. No destination snapshot, drawing readback, replay or
new shader program is required. Public resource controls pair both rectangle
ports and targets; driver controls cover capacity and transactional rejection.
Synthetic eight-overlap drift is diagnostic, not real-image qualification.

`R03-PRIM-FIRSTGEN-GUI-ALPHA-RECT` separately qualifies that same executor for
first-generation identity GUI. Both logical sizes equal the target raster,
scales are one, camera and offsets are zero, and maximisation is inactive.
The already resolved raster bounds are not transformed again. Existing non-GUI
controls and the uninitialized/nonidentity GUI exclusions remain unchanged.

Typed primitive entries consume raster coordinates already resolved by the builtin
view mapping; `common/gml_render_primitives.c::resident_flat_eligible` must not
apply or reject them based on the current world scale. The flat regions, filled
spans, line boxes and outline pixels below share this rule, qualified with inactive,
unit, reduced/nonuniform, enlarged/nonuniform and reflected world state. The
explicit logical circle preparation remains `common/gml_render_primitives.c::gml_render_circle_geometry`.
Geometry budgets and ordinary opaque state restrictions are unchanged.

R03-PRIM-LINE-ALPHA-NOOP is EXACT RGBA0: the three typed integer/colour/subpixel
line ports retain a resident pass when drawing alpha is nonpositive and no enabled
destination-colour blend can write. The unchanged CPU filled-box leaf already
performs no pixel write in this state. The common predicate preserves pending
fills, stale CPU pixels, resource authority and versions without submitting an
operation or reading the destination. A no-op is not credited as a GPU draw.
Positive alpha, NaN and enabled destination-colour blends retain the existing
executor and CPU-boundary policy; visible GUI line admission is not broadened.
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_line_alpha_noop`
checks all three ports, pending/committed prefixes and visible exclusions.
Device comparison R03-PRIM-LINE-ALPHA-NOOP pairs144 complete-RGBA controls
containing432 line calls, including SCREEN/owned/numbered targets and mapped GUI;
prefix/suffix bytes and explicitly sealed no-current bytes require EXACT0.

R03-PRIM-CIRCLE-ALPHA-NOOP is EXACT RGBA0: all four integer/subpixel uniform/
colour circle ports retain the same qualified nonpositive-alpha no-op, including
filled/outline and zero-radius calls. Their unchanged CPU pixel or line-box leaf
returns before writing; enabled destination-colour blends remain excluded.
The shared pure `resident_primitive_alpha_noop` predicate also retains the line
rule above. No visible circle admission, CPU body, operation count, resource
version or pending work is changed. No-op calls are not credited as GPU draws.
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_circle_alpha_noop`
requires authority/pending preservation and exact positive-alpha/destination-
colour write exclusions with matched target-alpha policies. Device comparison
R03-PRIM-CIRCLE-ALPHA-NOOP pairs144 full-RGBA controls containing2304 calls across
SCREEN/owned/numbered targets and mapped GUI; visible prefix/suffix and explicitly
sealed no-current bytes require EXACT0, with zero ordinary reads or snapshots.

The bounded destination-factor surface rule may skip a fully clipped rectangle
after a CPU suffix while the witnessed scene session remains active. This observes
neither source nor destination and changes no resource version. A visible rectangle
still retains that CPU suffix; it never resumes GPU writing through this exception.

R03-PRIM-RECT-COALESCED retains the unchanged CPU outline collector and admission
for already qualified opaque rectangles. Consecutive equal-colour edge pixels
form at most four disjoint regions, with the original8192 emitted-pixel limit.
The circle, triangle, partial-alpha and special-state paths are unchanged.

The rectangle function matrix supersedes the older rectangle rules' target,
generation, GUI, gradient and outline exclusions for normal full-write state with
an on-demand device owner. Their already-qualified execution bodies and controls
remain intact. Non-normal/custom draw state, phase planes and authored bounds
outside signed16 coordinates remain explicit CPU debt, not GPU execution credit.

The R03-P9-FILTERED-REAL-TEXT arithmetic has separate cached GLES programs for
first-generation and modern text. Each writes three GPU passes, one RGB channel
at a time; alpha is written only with blue. All passes sample the same bounded
pre-glyph device snapshot, preserving source-over order and alpha. Each complete
GLES glyph on a reported V3D renderer, or when the renderer identity is unavailable,
is submitted with `glFlush` before another pass can sample its target. This prevents
the measured lost RGB writes on translucent V3D sources without a completion wait or
CPU pixel read. Other identified renderers retain the ordered same-context command
stream without that per-glyph submission. The decision is cached per context and
recomputed at reset; arithmetic, snapshots and channel/alpha ordering are unchanged.
`scene_gles_font_programs_case` checks both submission policies, missing/empty identity
and V3D wrapped renderer strings with the same whole-batch/failure/channel controls.
The general
exact GLES program excludes font arithmetic; desktop GL retains its combined
single-pass program. Integer-limb shifts avoid undefined native shift counts,
and a bounded highest-bit search replaces repeated normalisation shifts without
changing rounding. This limits compiler inlining rather than moving text to the
CPU. `tests/unit/video/gpu/test_gpu.c::scene_gles_font_programs_case` covers channel
masks, cached generation selection and partial-pass failure cleanup; the existing
real-device filtered-font comparison remains the pixel qualification.

Surface metadata used to prepare these rules has a distinct non-raster owner:
`common/gml_render_surfaces.c::surface_dimensions` prepares
`::gml_surface_exists`, `::gml_surface_width` and `::gml_surface_height` from
backing/lifetime, extent and resource-authority metadata. These calls issue no GPU
draw or shader and do not consume journals or seal valid pending results. They
preserve invalid-resource and application-publication fences. Actual pixel reads
still use `cpu/gml_render_cpu_surfaces.c::surface_pixels_access` and
`common/gml_render_resources.c::gml_render_resource_acquire_cpu_read` /
`::gml_render_resource_checkpoint`.
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::surface_metadata_preserves_device_authority`
compares authority, versions, replay/checkpoint counts and real pixel-observer
results for application and numbered resources. This metadata rule removes an
unnecessary CPU observer, not the content-program checkpoint contract.

P8-T27 extends the existing `R03-P8-STATE-RETIRE` state/lifetime boundary, not a new raster
rule. `tests/contract/libretro_dynamic_rewind.c::monitor_growth_case` uses the same authored
moving-sprite/audio preparation at a 1920×1080 logical raster, then changes the delivered virtual
monitor to 1280×960. The unchanged CPU scene body and the existing GPU sprite executor/shader from
`R03-SPR-NEAREST` remain responsible for pixels; the test makes a completed frame high-entropy solely
to distinguish the 1-MiB fixed compact ring from acknowledged variable capacity. It checks
frontend size-before-save, no-context callbacks, exact policy-specific restore/continuation,
visible image/audio, accepted scene sprites, Reset and bounded uncompressed copy/restore cost.
Rejected/unsupported variable-size negotiation keeps the fixed slot; only an acknowledged frontend
may grow it. This does not accelerate state copying or assert all Draw/GUI is GPU-resident.

P8-T28 extends that same `R03-P8-STATE-RETIRE` lifetime rule through an unannounced context reset,
not a new GPU raster kernel. `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::context_replacement_reuses_names_case`
uses the unchanged `R03-SPR-NEAREST` CPU/GPU draw preparation, saves and restores with GL calls
forbidden, then resets the fake driver namespace and deliberately reuses an old texture number.
It compares the CPU and GPU-selected images and canonical states across the restored frame and
continuation, requires new GPU sprite work, and rejects deletion of the recycled live texture.
`::refused_context_case` separately covers a named missing entry point, CPU continuation and a
later valid GPU adoption. These are engine/fake-driver lifecycle controls; physical-device pixels
and final frontend cost retain their separately recorded evidence boundaries.

P8-T29 qualifies the per-engine part of `R03-P3-CONTEXT-RESET` and `R03-P8-STATE-RETIRE` without
adding a shader or CPU algorithm. `tests/integration/test_graphics_state.c::two_live_gpu_engines_case`
interleaves two GPU-selected engines and two CPU controls at different frame positions. Each GPU
engine owns a distinct host resolver result for `glGetError`, framebuffer callback, source texture,
scene counter and canonical state. Resetting then non-current-destroying only one engine must leave
the other's callback dispatch, resource identity, image, state and counted sprite work intact. The
fixture's shared fake driver proves logical isolation and entry-table ownership, not independent
physical GL contexts or final performance; T25's separate real-device comparison owns pixels.

## Frame CPU-access and resident-source audit

This is the live P7 frame-coherence map, not the historical P1 classification.
The class names describe the access actually performed, not the name of its
public API. A CPU read/write is ordered after queued scene work. The remaining
cost and capability limits are explicit below; a CPU fallback is not counted as
GPU execution.

| Access owner and grouped variants | Class and coherence boundary | Current limit or next owner |
|---|---|---|
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_begin`; `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_begin_retaining_target`; resident owned/base begins | Metadata and pass selection after scene/rotation flush; retained stack, fills and target identity are preserved or reject resident begin. `gml_render_begin_owned_application` is a CPU write with resource acquire/commit; `gml_render_begin_resident_owned_application`, `gml_render_begin_resident_base` and `gml_render_begin_resident_base_retaining_target` select the qualified device target. The retaining base variant preserves a positive classic Step target while replacing its base dimensions/camera. | Unsupported phase, interpolated, canvas and special states deliberately use the CPU pass. A new screen CPU consumer must materialise the prior deferred presentation before an ordinary begin resets it. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_application_surface_bind`, `::gml_render_application_surface_select_owned`, and `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_render_application_surface_publish` | Metadata; a changed binding flushes queued scene work. `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::application_surface_resize` is a CPU read with barrier before copying old pixels, then creates and binds new storage; `gml_render_application_surface_owned_clear` and `::gml_render_application_surface_owned_view` are CPU writes with acquire/commit. | Surface 0 can be borrowed, disabled or independently resized. Do not infer ownership from its numeric ID. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_application_surface_owned_read`, `::gml_render_target_pixel`; `common/gml_render_surfaces.c::gml_surface_pixels_read`; `cpu/gml_render_cpu_surfaces.c::surface_pixels_observe` | CPU reads with scene flush and resource acquire; target reads also resolve pending Draw/fill. `cpu/gml_render_cpu_surfaces.c::surface_pixels` is a CPU write with acquire/commit and suspended-fill realization. | Numbered and application resources remain coherent, but a non-replayable device result can require a full checkpoint. `gml_surface_exists`, `gml_surface_width` and `gml_surface_height` currently call `surface_pixels_observe`: although their results are metadata, their **implementation is a CPU read/barrier** and its ordinary-route cost belongs to P9. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_sample_planes_update`; renderer coverage/target metrics; immutable atlas and runtime image source selection | Metadata or read-only asset borrow, not a framebuffer borrow. The sample-plane pointers are attached to the pass; their producers write the classic/application phase arrays before the CPU sample that consumes them. | An asset reference does not make a mutable target current. Runtime page/version rules are separate P5/P6 rows above. |
| `cpu/gml_render_cpu_backend.c::gml_render_cpu_backend_prepare_draw_view`, `cpu/gml_render_cpu_primitives.c` particle leaves and `src/video/software3d/` draws | CPU writes through the resident CPU barrier and preserved backend draw view; numbered targets use their surface facade. Opt-in diagnostics mark actual software-3D color writes and count hidden-surface depth tests separately from ordinary particle/software draws. | Ordered CPU paths, not device kernels. Only actual depth-tested writes enter the depth exception counter; an active 3D flag alone grants no exception. `boundary.dispatch_accounting` compares both policies and diagnostics on/off pixels. The complete-load dispatch footer described in DIAGNOSTICS.md includes ordinary rasters, retained replay and composition; it is accounting, not a claim of zero fallback or driver qualification. |
| `src/core/engine_presentation.c::render_multiview_application`, `::seed_view_rect`, `::compose_view_rect`; `gml_render.c::gml_render_surface_mirror_pixels` | Per-view scratch is a CPU write/seed; each resident view is flushed and CPU-acquired before copy/composition. The mirror writes a numbered surface through its target facade before same-frame GUI readers. | Correctness is covered by R03-P7-MULTIVIEW-SEED/RETAINED/SURFACE. Whole-view readbacks and CPU composition remain P9 transfer debt. |
| `gml_render.c::gml_render_flush_pending_fill`, `::gml_render_flush_pending_underlay`; `common/gml_render_presentation.c::render_record_deferred_underlay`, `::render_present_first_generation`; rotated-batch flush | A qualified fill is an accelerated neutral clear; otherwise the target is acquired for a CPU write. Deferred terminal presentation records metadata and a frame-local source borrow; CPU execution acquires the resident source in `cpu/gml_render_cpu_presentation.c::gml_render_cpu_present_image`. R03-P9-AUTOMATIC-UNDERLAY instead composes an unmasked current application into the owned resident SCREEN before following atlas work, with byte-free exact source retention in R03-P9-REPLAY-SOURCE. The rotated queue flushes before a borrow or target change. First-generation mask-covered underlay still acquires the current CPU resource before its temporary alpha borrow and restores the original bytes without a version change. | R03-P7-UNDERLAY-ALPHA-BORROW remains the mask exclusion's GPU-current resource guard, not acceleration of that alpha borrow. Unqualified sources/state/geometry retain CPU composition; a non-replayable source uses its mandatory checkpoint. |
| `src/core/engine.c::engine_run_frame` world/view/GUI, centered-aspect crop, indirect-GUI scaler, empty-screen fallback; `src/core/engine_presentation.c::draw_game_cursor` and `::classic_transition_start` | World/GUI direct copies and scans follow the view or resident-screen CPU barrier. A complete current indirect-GUI SCREEN can defer its terminal pixel-centre enlargement without a CPU borrow through `gml_render_defer_resident_screen_upscale`; centered and excluded indirect GUI materialise before reading `fb`, `gui_buffer` or `screen`. Empty-screen fallback is skipped for a deferred full-target presentation; its surface-0 probe uses the CPU-read facade. Cursor and transition snapshot materialise the completed frame before their CPU draw/copy. | R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI maps the resident enlargement and its exact canonical replay. Centered GUI, reductions, CPU-written sources, host canvas/crop/shift, classic fade and CPU cursor retain their explicit fallback/composition boundaries. R03-P7-SCREEN-CPU-BORROW guards the late consumers. |
| `src/core/engine_presentation.c::resolve_host_frame`, `::engine_build_host_plan`, `::present_shift_frame`; `src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_present_hardware_canvas` | CPU read after `engine_materialize_completed_frame`, followed by CPU plan/copy or a complete-frame upload. Resident SCREEN and host-canvas presentations avoid it when their source and plan qualify. | Host canvas CRT/sharp-bilinear, nonresident box, crop, presentation shift and other nonresident plans still materialise/transport. These are measured P7 fallback policies and unresolved P9 ordinary-route transfer costs. |
| `src/core/engine_state.c::state_write_completed_frame`, `src/core/engine_presentation.c::log_present_pass`, coverage diagnostics | CPU read after explicit completed-frame materialisation or resource facade acquisition. Diagnostics that scan pixels are opt-in, not evidence of ordinary-route residency. | P8 must qualify frontend state/ring call ordering; P9 measures materialisation cost. |

`GmlPlanImage.cpu_pixels` is a frame-local CPU borrow owned by the caller; a
retained host plan remains valid only while its owner retains that allocation.
`GmlPlanImage.resident_image` is a context-local resource reference with
incarnation and content version. Today `common/gml_render_plan.c::gml_render_plan_validate`
admits it for an opaque APPLICATION/application-surface or SCREEN/completed-frame
`BLIT_OPAQUE_NEAREST` source, with matching typed class and current version;
`gpu/opengl/gml_render_gpu_opengl_plan.c::gml_gpu_opengl_execute` resolves the
exact current texture, while `cpu/gml_render_cpu_plan.c::gml_render_cpu_plan_execute_pooled`
refuses a resident-only image. The selected resident underlay path is
`gml_render.c::gml_render_defer_resident_underlay` into
`src/core/engine_graphics.c::engine_present_hardware_screen`, with CPU replay
through `gml_render_cpu_present_image` when the device is unavailable. Direct completed SCREEN
presentation and `gml_render_defer_resident_screen_upscale` use the same typed plan;
their canonical observers resolve the SCREEN resource, not the application substitute.

| Presentation source and operation | Device capability today | Correct fallback and remaining owner |
|---|---|---|
| Opaque owned application, deferred nearest underlay | Resident application image → exact-version GPU nearest plan; no complete source upload | CPU materialise/replay on refusal or context loss. |
| Unmasked current owned application, automatic underlay before filtered SCREEN/GUI atlas work | R03-P9-AUTOMATIC-UNDERLAY uses the existing ordered exact surface quad. A witnessed on-demand device may upload an already committed CPU-current source at 1:1 extents without reading SCREEN; legacy exact recipes and GPU-source scaling remain unchanged. | Scaled CPU-current source, mask/phase, special state, open source writer, unavailable witness, borrowed/stale source or excluded footprint remains the original CPU compositor. Resident pixels still require observation at explicit consumers; no automatic frame checkpoint is implied. |
| Current completed SCREEN or writer-free indirect GUI with nonshrinking axes | Typed resident SCREEN → exact-version GPU nearest plan, using the existing axis-map sampler without complete CPU transport | CPU materialise/replay on device refusal/loss; a genuine writer invalidates residence. Enlargement canonical pixels retain their original zero XRGB high byte. |
| Borrowed/disabled application without a current resident target, multiview scratch and excluded indirect GUI | Only separately qualified scene draws and current source versions are GPU work | CPU barrier/composition and complete presentation transport; reduction, writer suffix, centered aspect, canvas, shift and crop remain explicit exclusions, not completed P9 residency. |
| CPU-current application or completed frame, nearest/box/sharp-bilinear host plan | CPU source upload or CPU plan; box/sharp require their established CPU kernel to preserve exact pixels | P7 fallback correctness is measured. Resident SCREEN box follows R03-P9-RESIDENT-HOST-FIT (TOLERANCE±2); sharp/crop/shift remain open. |
| Content shader terminal plan | GPU program when qualified, with a checkpoint/readback where required | Ineligible program uses the documented CPU/plain path. P7-S1-D has a qualified natural shader/original comparison; P8 checks state ordering and P9 costs. |

The same `resident_identity_screen_gui` predicate also admits an identity-geometry sprite on a
direct, disabled-application GUI `SCREEN` target. `engine_run_frame` already selected
`gml_render_begin_resident_base` for that target before R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI; its sprite now follows
`resident_integer_sprite` / `gml_render_resident_atlas_crop`, the original
`gml_render_cpu_blit_sprite_ext_resolved` fallback, the counted textured-quad executor and
`R03_SHADER_DIRECT_TEXTURE` branch named in that row. The direct variant in
`resident_indirect_gui_case` compares full CPU/scene output bytes, a visible sprite, counted clear
and textured-quad work, repeated frames and context loss. This does not qualify arbitrary direct
GUI operations: transformed GUI geometry retains its CPU path. The additional
R03-P9-GUI-FILTERED-ATLAS row maps identity-screen filtered atlas preparation,
screen alpha policy and real shader branches. R03-P9-GUI-MAPPED-SPRITE additionally maps
owned logical GUI scaling to the unchanged ordinary/part and distinct stretched sprite
rules, including their alpha and checkpoint/replay limits. R03-RESIDENT-PRESENT separately qualifies
direct completed SCREEN presentation, and R03-P7-INDIRECT-GUI maps the nonshrinking
writer-free terminal enlargement; neither follows merely from sprite counts. The
R03-P7-VIEWLESS-BASE row's former broad indirect-GUI exclusion is superseded only for these
qualified GUI rules; its viewless-world and borrowed-surface-0 claims are unchanged.
R03-P9-DIRECT-AUTOMATIC-GUI additionally prepares the direct SCREEN for a viewless or single-view
nonclassic owned application with automatic drawing enabled. Its pending underlay
and first qualified stretched draw consume the existing surface and sprite rules,
not an application-image substitute. Classic and multiview direct admission remain
unchanged; covered-alpha canonical checkpoints and excluded writers remain explicit.
R03-P9-PRE-DRAW-SCREEN admits the existing SCREEN resource for nonclassic,
noncomposed, unforced OpenGL Pre-Draw. The implicit clear remains EXACT; draws
retain their existing per-family EXACT or TOLERANCE limits and checked barriers.
The event still precedes view selection and its queue flushes before geometry
updates. Pending state, stacked targets and unavailable resources retain checked
CPU fallback. `test_graphics_state.c::resident_pre_draw_screen_case` pairs the
CPU and selected paths, including the event's view mutation, zero ordinary CPU
work and exact canonical state. `test_gpu_scene_route --keep-pre-draw` exports
the same neutral route for real-device comparison. It does not qualify previously excluded sprites
or establish complete real-device image coverage.
CPU materialization of a deferred presentation publishes its completed target,
retiring any earlier SCREEN resource. A later canonical observer must not replay
that resource's preceding clear over the newly materialized image. This ownership
transition adds no per-frame readback and does not alter numbered-surface alpha.
The historical R03-BG-DIRECT-NEAREST-COPY row's additive exclusion is superseded for the
qualified additive subset by R03-BG-ADDITIVE. Other additive variants still take its stated
ordered CPU fallback.

The neutral scene-op ledger classifies its unselected dedicated triangle opcode
with the real CPU fallback; its name must not be used to infer that the
qualified positioned quads are absent.
Conversely, resident texture sampling and flat-region primitive paint do not imply complete P6
primitive/3D coverage or P7 shaders/effects are delivered. Any later qualification updates
the affected row or adds a rule group when its actual backend branch and
comparison are available.

## Function dispatch inventory (D8 foundation, 2026-10-07)

This inventory is maintained by `tests/architecture/check_render_operations.py`, already
part of `make architecture-check`. It discovers public drawing facades, resident
preparators, software raster/vertex/model entries and the existing OpenGL draw boundaries
from their C definitions, independently of these rows. It rejects missing/extra entries,
a changed submission route, or a changed definition without an updated row.
The revision is a SHA-256 of C tokens excluding comments, not a pixel expectation.

`PARTIAL` means only that a conservative call graph reaches the named typed admission
port. It does **not** mean that the draw is admitted in every state, that the device accepts
it, or that a whole game is GPU-resident. CPU preparation/observation/flush boundaries
are cut from the graph: completing a prior GPU prefix never credits a later CPU writer.
`DEBT` records an ordinary CPU drawing route. `HELPER` records the enumerated existing
admission/CPU-handoff utilities, not an exception that may hide a new drawing API.
`DEFERRED` records the two existing host-presentation adapters. `DEVICE` records
execution/encoding boundaries, not complete support for every payload.

This is the function-level foundation, **not yet the complete D8 gate**. Builtin API
adapters/aliases, resolved packet/shader variants and their complete paired CPU/GPU
control bindings still require mechanical coverage before D8 is declared complete.
The existing R03 map above remains the sole owner of qualified variant behavior,
EXACT/TOLERANCE bounds and known exclusions. No renderer policy rule is claimed to
be enforced by this inventory alone.

R03-TEXT-NEAREST-FUNCTION requires complete nearest public-string paired proof
before qualification. EXACT RGBA0 covers opaque/binary endpoints and disabled
replacement; partial source/draw alpha and inverse blend require TOLERANCE±2
with exact coverage. The bounded immutable atlas owner offers signed nearest
scales, all ordinary destinations, Classic/Studio, mapped world/GUI, tint and
raw cutout without a new shader, destination snapshot, ordinary observation or
CPU replay. Existing cheap/runtime packets stay first. Detached real glyphs
use conservative alpha metadata; copied sprite cells retain their distinct
CPU cache phase. `common/gml_render_text.c::draw_text_real_plain` and
`common/gml_render_text.c::draw_text_transformed_font` preserve the CPU layout;
`common/gml_render_sprites.c::gml_render_resident_font_glyph` and
`common/gml_render_sprites.c::gml_render_resident_sprite_font_glyph` prepare
cells through the existing atlas packet. `cpu/gml_render_cpu_blit.c::blit`
remains the reference; `gpu/opengl/gml_gpu_opengl_batch.c::integer_function_sprite_valid`
admits the bounded packet. The admission/authority unit
`tests/unit/video/renderer/test_render_resources.c::resident_nearest_text_function`
does not prove pixels: the public-string device comparison must establish the
font/source/draw-alpha/tint/cutout/blend/scale/layout/cache matrix and rejecting
negatives, ordinary zero transport and exact detached actual bytes; a subset
or admission pass cannot qualify the rule. The new owner
rejects active maximised/offset GUI and copied cells whose physical and logical
dimensions differ. Existing qualified owners or ordered CPU debt remain for
these cases; this does not assert that they cannot meet D2. Other blends,
filter/rotation, runtime/SDF/subpixel, shaders/masks/fog/depth, auxiliary planes,
oversized geometry and unresolved GUI authority/presentation remain explicit
ordered CPU debt, not an assertion that those variants cannot meet D2.
